### MRD-RAG: Enhancing Medical Diagnosis with Multi-Round Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07724v1)
- **Authors**: Yixiang Chen, Penglei Sun, Xiang Li, Xiaowen Chu
- **Abstract**: In recent years, accurately and quickly deploying medical large language models (LLMs) has become a significant trend. Among these, retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) has garnered significant attention due to its features of rapid deployment and privacy protection. However, existing medical RAG frameworks still have shortcomings. Most existing medical RAG frameworks are designed for single-round question answering tasks and are not suitable for multi-round diagnostic dialogue. On the other hand, existing medical multi-round RAG frameworks do not consider the interconnections between potential diseases to inquire precisely like a doctor. To address these issues, we propose a Multi-Round Diagnostic RAG (MRD-RAG) framework that mimics the doctor's diagnostic process. This RAG framework can analyze diagnosis information of potential diseases and accurately conduct multi-round diagnosis like a doctor. To evaluate the effectiveness of our proposed frameworks, we conduct experiments on two modern medical datasets and two traditional Chinese medicine datasets, with evaluations by GPT and human doctors on different methods. The results indicate that our RAG framework can significantly enhance the diagnostic performance of LLMs, highlighting the potential of our approach in medical diagnosis. The code and data can be found in our project website https://github.com/YixiangCh/MRD-RAG/tree/master.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MRD-RAG (Multi-Round Diagnostic Retrieval-Augmented Generation), a framework designed to enhance medical diagnosis using large language models (LLMs).  The key idea is to mimic the diagnostic process of human doctors by engaging in multi-round dialogues with patients, analyzing diagnostic information of potential diseases, and proactively inquiring about relevant symptoms.  MRD-RAG incorporates a custom-built knowledge base consisting of both modern medicine (MM) and traditional Chinese medicine (TCM) diseases organized into a tree structure (DI-Tree). It also uses a "pseudo medical history" to bridge the semantic gap between patients' utterances and disease information. The framework comprises three modules: a retriever (accessing the DI-Tree), an analyzer (summarizing disease interconnections), and a doctor (posing questions or making diagnoses). Experimental results on MM and TCM datasets, evaluated by GPT-4 mini and human doctors, indicate that MRD-RAG improves diagnostic performance compared to LLMs without RAG and with single-round RAG.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach by focusing on multi-round diagnostic dialogues and explicitly modeling the interconnectedness of potential diseases.  The construction of the DI-Tree, including both MM and TCM knowledge, and the use of pseudo medical history are also innovative aspects.  Existing RAG-based medical systems often focus on single-round interactions or don't model disease relationships as explicitly.
*   **Significance:** Improving the diagnostic accuracy of LLMs has significant practical implications for healthcare.  The focus on both modern and traditional Chinese medicine makes the approach potentially relevant in diverse cultural contexts. The reported improvements over baseline methods, particularly as assessed by human doctors, suggest a potentially real-world impact.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing medical RAG frameworks.
    *   The proposed MRD-RAG framework is well-motivated and logically structured.
    *   The creation of the DI-Tree knowledge base and the use of pseudo medical history demonstrate practical engineering efforts.
    *   The experimental evaluation includes both automated metrics (GPT) and human evaluations, strengthening the validity of the findings.
    *   The case study provides concrete examples of how MRD-RAG operates in practice.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on GPT-4 mini for automated evaluation is a potential limitation. While powerful, GPT's diagnostic capabilities may not perfectly align with human doctors, particularly in TCM.  It would be better to have more doctor-based analysis.
    *   While improvements are reported, the absolute performance gains, especially for TCM diagnosis seem relatively small, suggesting room for improvement.  The overall scale and thoroughness of the human evaluations (only 200 cases) could be increased to strengthen the statistical significance of the findings.
    *   The detailed analysis of the hyperparameters (e.g., top-k) is limited. A more thorough hyperparameter optimization study could further improve the framework's performance.
    *   The generalizability of the framework may be a concern. The DI-Tree knowledge base is specific to MM and TCM.  Adapting the approach to other medical domains might require substantial effort.
    *   The paper lacks a comprehensive discussion of limitations. For example, how does the system perform with complex multi-morbidity patients? Or, how do you handle biased medical data?

* **Impact**: The work makes a solid step toward improving the usability of LLMs in medical diagnosis.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed RAG framework for medical diagnosis. The focus on multi-round dialogue, disease relationships, and the incorporation of TCM knowledge are valuable contributions.  The experimental results, while not groundbreaking, suggest that MRD-RAG can improve diagnostic accuracy. However, the limitations in the evaluation, the relatively small performance gains, and the lack of more detailed analysis temper enthusiasm somewhat. The method is unlikely to be immediately transformative but advances the field.

### Automated Construction of a Knowledge Graph of Nuclear Fusion Energy for Effective Elicitation and Retrieval of Information
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07738v1)
- **Authors**: A. Loreti, K. Chen, R. George, R. Firth, A. Agnello, S. Tanaka
- **Abstract**: In this document, we discuss a multi-step approach to automated construction of a knowledge graph, for structuring and representing domain-specific knowledge from large document corpora. We apply our method to build the first knowledge graph of nuclear fusion energy, a highly specialized field characterized by vast scope and heterogeneity. This is an ideal benchmark to test the key features of our pipeline, including automatic named entity recognition and entity resolution. We show how pre-trained large language models can be used to address these challenges and we evaluate their performance against Zipf's law, which characterizes human-generated natural language. Additionally, we develop a knowledge-graph retrieval-augmented generation system that combines large language models with a multi-prompt approach. This system provides contextually relevant answers to natural-language queries, including complex multi-hop questions that require reasoning across interconnected entities.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper based on its content.

**Summary**

The paper presents a method for automatically constructing a knowledge graph (KG) specifically for nuclear fusion energy. It addresses the challenge of structuring and representing domain-specific knowledge from large document corpora, a task made difficult by the vast scope and heterogeneity of the fusion energy field. The approach involves several automated steps, including data acquisition, named entity recognition (NER) using Large Language Models (LLMs), entity resolution, KG construction, and relation extraction. Furthermore, the authors develop a KG Retrieval-Augmented Generation (KG-RAG) system, combining the knowledge graph with LLMs to provide contextually relevant answers to natural-language queries. The goal is to enhance data accessibility, facilitate interaction between human understanding and machine processing, and minimize LLM hallucination.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the creation of a *domain-specific* knowledge graph for nuclear fusion energy. While the individual components (NER, KG construction, RAG) are well-established techniques, their application and integration within this specific domain, to my knowledge, represents a novel contribution. The authors explicitly state the lack of existing KGs for nuclear fusion. Furthermore, the use of a multi-prompt approach in conjunction with LLMs for the various stages (NER, entity resolution, relation extraction) seems to provide some incremental innovation. The application of LLMs, particularly Llama3, for this specific purpose also adds a layer of innovation. The investigation of the application of Zipf's law to assess entity resolution quality also represents a useful novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in its potential to:

    *   **Improve Information Retrieval:** A structured KG can enhance search and retrieval of information related to nuclear fusion, facilitating research and development.
    *   **Facilitate Collaboration:** A unified KB can foster collaboration among researchers, funding agencies, and policy makers.
    *   **Enable Reasoning:** The KG-RAG system can provide contextually relevant answers to complex multi-hop questions, enabling reasoning across interconnected entities.
    *   **Address LLM Limitations:** The integration of KG aims to address the limitation of LLMs in hallucinating information.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper describes the pipeline in detail.
    *   **Use of State-of-the-Art Techniques:** The paper utilizes LLMs (Llama3) and a declarative query language (Cypher).
    *   **Evaluation:** The paper reports results and evaluation of both the KG construction and KG-RAG system.
    *   **Practical Application:** The system created has a practical application for a very specialized field, with implications for research and development.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation Scope:** While the paper presents some evaluation, the study of RAG system performance needs to be more comprehensive. The evaluation metrics need to be better defined and quantified. The lack of evaluation of the complete KG generation process is also a significant limitation.
    *   **Lack of Baselines:** The paper lacks comparison with baseline systems for NER, entity resolution, and KG construction. This makes it difficult to assess the true performance gain from using LLMs and the proposed approach.
    *   **Limited Discussion on Semantic Relationship Extraction:** The description of how the semantic relationships are identified between entities is fairly brief and needs more detail.
    *   **Clarity on Prompting:** The paper mentions a multi-prompt approach. What does this entail? Some discussion/clarification here would be appreciated.
    *   **The accuracy of the entity categorisation seems quite high, but not tested against any standard baseline.**
    *   **No discussion of the limitations of the approach, which is concerning.**

*   **Potential Influence:** The KG-RAG system developed has the potential to be used as a basis for more advanced applications in the field of nuclear fusion, such as knowledge discovery, decision support, and automated design.

*   **Justification of Score:** The paper presents a novel application of established techniques to a specific and important domain. While the individual components are not ground-breaking, their integration and application to nuclear fusion energy represents a significant contribution. However, the somewhat limited evaluation, lack of comparisons to baselines, and incomplete discussion of limitations affect the overall impact.

### Zero-Shot Cross-Domain Code Search without Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07740v1)
- **Authors**: Keyu Liang, Zhongxin Liu, Chao Liu, Zhiyuan Wan, David Lo, Xiaohu Yang
- **Abstract**: Code search aims to retrieve semantically relevant code snippets for natural language queries. While pre-trained language models (PLMs) have shown remarkable performance in this task, they struggle in cross-domain scenarios, often requiring costly fine-tuning or facing performance drops in zero-shot settings. RAPID, which generates synthetic data for model fine-tuning, is currently the only effective method for zero-shot cross-domain code search. Despite its effectiveness, RAPID demands substantial computational resources for fine-tuning and needs to maintain specialized models for each domain, underscoring the need for a zero-shot, fine-tuning-free approach for cross-domain code search. The key to tackling zero-shot cross-domain code search lies in bridging the gaps among domains. In this work, we propose to break the query-code matching process of code search into two simpler tasks: query-comment matching and code-code matching. Our empirical study reveals the strong complementarity among the three matching schemas in zero-shot cross-domain settings, i.e., query-code, query-comment, and code-code matching. Based on the findings, we propose CodeBridge, a zero-shot, fine-tuning-free approach for cross-domain code search. Specifically, CodeBridge uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate comments and pseudo-code, then combines query-code, query-comment, and code-code matching via PLM-based similarity scoring and sampling-based fusion. Experimental results show that our approach outperforms the state-of-the-art PLM-based code search approaches, i.e., CoCoSoDa and UniXcoder, by an average of 21.4% and 24.9% in MRR, respectively, across three datasets. Our approach also yields results that are better than or comparable to those of the zero-shot cross-domain code search approach RAPID, which requires costly fine-tuning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents CodeBridge, a zero-shot, fine-tuning-free approach for cross-domain code search. It addresses the challenge of domain gaps that hinder the performance of pre-trained language models (PLMs) when applied to code search in new programming languages or domains without fine-tuning. CodeBridge decomposes the query-code matching process into two simpler tasks: query-comment matching and code-code matching. It utilizes large language models (LLMs) to generate comments for code snippets and code snippets for queries. It then encodes queries, code, generated comments, and generated code into embeddings using PLMs and computes similarities through the three matching schemas: query-code, query-comment, and generated code-code. Finally, it employs a sampling-based fusion approach to combine these similarity scores. The authors demonstrate experimentally that CodeBridge outperforms state-of-the-art PLM-based code search methods in zero-shot settings, including CoCoSoDa and UniXcoder, and achieves comparable or better performance than RAPID, a method that requires fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel and pragmatic approach to cross-domain code search. Decomposing the search process into simpler query-comment and code-code matching is a key innovation. Leveraging LLMs for comment and code generation to facilitate these matches in a zero-shot setting is also clever.  While the individual components (LLMs, embeddings, similarity measures, fusion) are not entirely new, their combination and application to this specific problem exhibit significant novelty. The approach of *avoiding* fine-tuning altogether for cross-domain scenarios represents a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial problem in code search: adapting to new domains without requiring costly fine-tuning.  The zero-shot capability of CodeBridge makes it highly practical, especially for low-resource programming languages or specific domains where labeled data is scarce. The fact that it performs competitively with, and in some cases outperforms, a fine-tuning-based approach (RAPID) highlights its significance. The demonstration of how query-comment and code-code matching can complement traditional query-code matching provides a new perspective on code search architectures. However, a significant amount of information is delegated to the LLMs, so if the LLM's performance is degraded then the entire model is degraded.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Novel decomposition of the code search task.
    *   Effective use of LLMs for comment and code generation.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets and baselines.
    *   Detailed ablation studies and sensitivity analysis.
    *   Insightful analysis of the strengths and weaknesses of different matching schemas.
    *   The paper's analysis of computational efficiency provides a balanced perspective.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Reliance on LLMs: The performance is heavily reliant on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs for code and comment generation. If the LLMs are not sufficiently competent for a given domain, the performance may suffer.

    *   Limited Generalizability Analysis: While the study tested performance across SQL, Solidity and CoSQA, a more thorough exploration of a wider range of programming languages and domain-specific datasets would reinforce the claim of domain agnosticism.
    *   Potential Bias: Even in a zero-shot setting, if the LLM has seen parts of the dataset during its training, some amount of bias may be present in results.
    *   "Fake" Matching: The model isn't matching to real comments or code. To increase the usefulness of the generated data, more attention should be paid to the generated comment's and code's quality.

*   **Impact:**  This paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of code search by providing a practical and effective zero-shot cross-domain solution. The findings could influence the design of future code search systems, especially those targeting low-resource domains or scenarios where rapid adaptation is required. It also highlights the potential of LLMs beyond fine-tuning for code-related tasks.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

CodeBridge presents a significant advance in zero-shot cross-domain code search, showcasing a creative approach to mitigate domain gaps. The approach is practical and well-validated through comprehensive experiments, and offers insights into the role of comments and generated code in assisting code searches. The major drawback is the reliance on and potential bias from the underlying LLMs. While it beats existing approaches that do not fine-tune and has a comparable accuracy against fine-tuning approaches, the reliance of a separate LLM is a strong restriction. These limitations, prevent a higher score, the innovation of *avoiding* the fine-tuning step entirely, as well as the impact it could have on low-resource code domains, warrants an 8.
- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### SF2T: Self-supervised Fragment Finetuning of Video-LLMs for Fine-Grained Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07745v1)
- **Authors**: Yangliu Hu, Zikai Song, Na Feng, Yawei Luo, Junqing Yu, Yi-Ping Phoebe Chen, Wei Yang
- **Abstract**: Video-based Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) have witnessed substantial advancements in recent years, propelled by the advancement in multi-modal LLMs. Although these models have demonstrated proficiency in providing the overall description of videos, they struggle with fine-grained understanding, particularly in aspects such as visual dynamics and video details inquiries. To tackle these shortcomings, we find that fine-tuning Video-LLMs on self-supervised fragment tasks, greatly improve their fine-grained video understanding abilities. Hence we propose two key contributions:(1) Self-Supervised Fragment Fine-Tuning (SF$^2$T), a novel effortless fine-tuning method, employs the rich inherent characteristics of videos for training, while unlocking more fine-grained understanding ability of Video-LLMs. Moreover, it relieves researchers from labor-intensive annotations and smartly circumvents the limitations of natural language, which often fails to capture the complex spatiotemporal variations in videos; (2) A novel benchmark dataset, namely FineVidBench, for rigorously assessing Video-LLMs' performance at both the scene and fragment levels, offering a comprehensive evaluation of their capabilities. We assessed multiple models and validated the effectiveness of SF$^2$T on them. Experimental results reveal that our approach improves their ability to capture and interpret spatiotemporal details.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper "SF2T: Self-supervised Fragment Finetuning of Video-LLMs for Fine-Grained Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of current Video-LLMs in fine-grained video understanding, particularly regarding visual dynamics and detailed inquiries. The authors propose Self-Supervised Fragment Fine-Tuning (SF2T), a novel method that fine-tunes Video-LLMs on self-supervised fragment tasks, leveraging the inherent characteristics of videos for training. This avoids labor-intensive annotations and circumvents the limitations of natural language.  The authors also introduce FineVidBench, a new benchmark dataset for evaluating Video-LLMs' performance at both scene and fragment levels. Experimental results demonstrate that SF2T improves the ability of models to capture and interpret spatiotemporal details.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the introduction of the SF2T fine-tuning strategy and the FineVidBench benchmark. The *idea* of self-supervision is not entirely new in the field of video understanding, but the *specific application* of fragment-based self-supervision to Video-LLMs, along with the suite of designed tasks (Counting, Consistency Verification, etc.), adds a layer of innovation. FineVidBench, while using existing video datasets, provides a novel evaluation framework tailored for fine-grained understanding.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from addressing a clear and current gap in Video-LLM capabilities.  The ability to understand fine-grained details is crucial for many real-world applications (surveillance, VR, autonomous driving). The elimination of manual annotation through SF2T is also a significant advantage, lowering the cost and complexity of training data creation. The comprehensive evaluation approach of FineVidBench offers a standardized way to assess and compare different models in this domain. The positive experimental results support the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Definition:** Clearly identifies a relevant and important problem in Video-LLMs.
    *   **Method:** SF2T is a well-defined and theoretically sound approach, leveraging inherent video properties for self-supervision.
    *   **Benchmark:**  FineVidBench provides a valuable resource for the community, enabling more rigorous evaluation of Video-LLMs.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of SF2T on various models and benchmarks. Ablation studies offer insights into the key components of the approach.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method, experimental setup, and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** While SF2T shows promise, the gains aren't *massive*. The improvement, though consistent, is not a complete solution to the fine-grained understanding problem. The paper acknowledges SF2T is more of a complimentary approach to SFT instead of a replacement, which somewhat limits its perceived transformative impact.
    *   **Dataset Dependence:** The performance may be influenced by the specific characteristics of the datasets used in FineVidBench. More evaluation across a wider variety of video types and tasks would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Comparison with SFT:** While comparisons with "Base" and "Base(SFT)" are provided, more detailed ablations comparing the *types* of data and tasks used in the existing SFT and SF2T are needed to have more clear insight into which are the specific benefits of the self-supervised approach, compared to more annotation.
    *   **Frame input count sensitivity:** The right side of table 2 shows that, as the frame input count increases, the accuracy generally decreases across models.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in Video-LLMs by promoting self-supervised fine-tuning approaches and providing a valuable benchmark for evaluating fine-grained understanding. Other researchers will be able to use FineVidBench to assess their models and adapt SF2T for other applications or architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a relevant problem in the field of Video-LLMs. The introduction of SF2T and FineVidBench is a valuable contribution. However, the gains observed are not revolutionary, and further validation across diverse datasets and tasks would strengthen the claims. The limitations regarding the direct comparison of SF2T and SFT are also a slight drawback. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects the positive contributions while acknowledging the areas for further improvement.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Virtual-mask Informed Prior for Sparse-view Dual-Energy CT Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07753v1)
- **Authors**: Zini Chen, Yao Xiao, Junyan Zhang, Shaoyu Wang, Liu Shi, Qiegen Liu
- **Abstract**: Sparse-view sampling in dual-energy computed tomography (DECT) significantly reduces radiation dose and increases imaging speed, yet is highly prone to artifacts. Although diffusion models have demonstrated potential in effectively handling incomplete data, most existing methods in this field focus on the image do-main and lack global constraints, which consequently leads to insufficient reconstruction quality. In this study, we propose a dual-domain virtual-mask in-formed diffusion model for sparse-view reconstruction by leveraging the high inter-channel correlation in DECT. Specifically, the study designs a virtual mask and applies it to the high-energy and low-energy data to perform perturbation operations, thus constructing high-dimensional tensors that serve as the prior information of the diffusion model. In addition, a dual-domain collaboration strategy is adopted to integrate the information of the randomly selected high-frequency components in the wavelet domain with the information in the projection domain, for the purpose of optimizing the global struc-tures and local details. Experimental results indicated that the present method exhibits excellent performance across multiple datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method, Virtual-mask Informed Prior (VIP-DECT), for sparse-view dual-energy computed tomography (DECT) reconstruction.  VIP-DECT leverages the correlation between high-energy and low-energy channels by using randomly generated virtual masks to perturb the data in both the projection and wavelet domains. This creates high-dimensional tensors, which serve as prior information for a diffusion model. A dual-domain collaborative strategy combines information from the projection domain (global features) and the wavelet domain's high-frequency components (local details) to optimize the reconstruction. The experimental results on head and mouse thoracic DECT datasets demonstrate excellent performance compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel elements:

*   **Virtual Mask Strategy:**  The use of virtual masks generated from Gaussian distributions to perturb data and create high-dimensional tensors is a creative approach to incorporating prior information into a diffusion model. The paper also explores the "Inverse Virtual Mask Cross-energy Transformation" i.e reversing the data perturbation process which could also be helpful from data consistency point of view.

*   **Dual-Domain Collaboration:** Combining information from the projection and wavelet domains is a promising strategy. This approach seems well-suited to exploiting the complementary strengths of each domain (global structure vs. local details). The combination helps resolve the issue of artefacts and incomplete details in the sparsely sampled DECT data.

**Significance:**

*   **Sparse-View DECT:** The problem of sparse-view DECT reconstruction is significant because it directly relates to reducing radiation dose and increasing imaging speed, critical for clinical applications. By producing excellent results, it can improve image quality with limited source data.

*   **Diffusion Models:** The application of diffusion models to this problem is a welcome trend. This approach offers potentially superior handling of incomplete data compared to traditional methods.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides thorough experimental results on multiple datasets (both synthetic and real). The use of PSNR, SSIM, and MSE, combined with visual comparisons and profile analysis, makes a strong case for the method's effectiveness.
*   **Ablation Study:**  The ablation study that examines the impact of mask perturbation ratios and the contributions of individual components (PPM and WPM) provides valuable insights into the design choices.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:**  The paper admits that the computation time is relatively long due to the diffusion model and dual-domain collaboration. While performance gains are significant, practicality in a clinical setting is strongly dependent on reducing the computational burden.  This needs further elaboration, possibly with benchmark times.
*   **Limited Generalization Evidence:** While the mouse thoracic dataset provides some evidence of generalization, more diverse datasets (e.g., different anatomical regions, data from different scanners) would strengthen the claim of robustness. More details on the specific parameters used for training different models is also missing and affects the reproducibility of the results.
*   **Clarity of Implementation Details:** Some of the details of the network architecture and training procedure could be made more explicit. This is especially important for diffusion models, where small details can significantly affect performance.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and promising approach with strong experimental results, there are limitations in terms of computational cost and evidence of generalization. The innovation surrounding the virtual masks and dual-domain collaboration makes the work novel within DECT reconstruction. It also presents strong quantitative and qualitative results on standard metrics. However, the high computational complexity and the lack of generalizability evidence makes the paper lacking in overall novelty and significance compared to other similar techniques in DECT literature. Therefore, I give a score of 6.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Efficient Tuning of Large Language Models for Knowledge-Grounded Dialogue Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07754v1)
- **Authors**: Bo Zhang, Hui Ma, Dailin Li, Jian Ding, Jian Wang, Bo Xu, HongFei Lin
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) demonstrate remarkable text comprehension and generation capabilities but often lack the ability to utilize up-to-date or domain-specific knowledge not included in their training data. To address this gap, we introduce KEDiT, an efficient method for fine-tuning LLMs for knowledge-grounded dialogue generation. KEDiT operates in two main phases: first, it employs an information bottleneck to compress retrieved knowledge into learnable parameters, retaining essential information while minimizing computational overhead. Second, a lightweight knowledge-aware adapter integrates these compressed knowledge vectors into the LLM during fine-tuning, updating less than 2\% of the model parameters. The experimental results on the Wizard of Wikipedia and a newly constructed PubMed-Dialog dataset demonstrate that KEDiT excels in generating contextually relevant and informative responses, outperforming competitive baselines in automatic, LLM-based, and human evaluations. This approach effectively combines the strengths of pretrained LLMs with the adaptability needed for incorporating dynamic knowledge, presenting a scalable solution for fields such as medicine.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Efficient Tuning of Large Language Models for Knowledge-Grounded Dialogue Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of incorporating up-to-date or domain-specific knowledge into large language models (LLMs) for dialogue generation. The authors propose KEDiT (Knowledge-Efficient Dialogue Tuning), a two-phase method that efficiently fine-tunes LLMs for knowledge-grounded dialogue. First, KEDiT employs an information bottleneck to compress retrieved knowledge into a set of learnable vectors, retaining essential information while minimizing computational overhead. Second, it integrates these compressed knowledge vectors into the LLM using a lightweight knowledge-aware adapter (KA-Adapter), updating only a small percentage of the model parameters. Experimental results on Wizard of Wikipedia and a newly constructed PubMed-Dialog dataset demonstrate that KEDiT excels in generating contextually relevant and informative responses, outperforming competitive baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a relevant and significant problem:** LLMs often lack the ability to utilize current or domain-specific knowledge not included in their training data, limiting their applicability in many real-world scenarios. KEDiT offers a practical solution to this issue.
*   **Efficient Fine-tuning:** KEDiT fine-tunes only a small percentage of the parameters (less than 2%), making it computationally efficient and scalable compared to end-to-end training approaches.
*   **Information Bottleneck:** Using an information bottleneck principle for knowledge compression is a clever idea to retain essential information while reducing computational cost.
*   **KA-Adapter:** The integration of compressed knowledge through a lightweight adapter is a sound design choice, preserving the pre-trained knowledge of the LLM.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides thorough experiments on both open-domain (Wizard of Wikipedia) and specialized domain (PubMed-Dialog) datasets. The evaluation includes automatic metrics, LLM-based evaluation, and human evaluations.
*   **Cross-Model Generalization Analysis:** Demonstrates generalizability across different LLMs such as Llama-3, Qwen2.5, and Mistral-v0.3.
*   **New Dataset:** The creation and use of the PubMed-Dialog dataset is a valuable contribution.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on Retrieved Knowledge:** The approach relies on the quality of retrieved knowledge. If the retrieval mechanism fails to provide relevant knowledge, KEDiT's performance will be negatively affected.
*   **Limited Improvement in Fluency:** While KEDiT excels in relevance and informativeness, the improvement in fluency is not as significant, possibly because baselines are already strong in this aspect.
*   **Complexity:** The two-phase approach introduces additional complexity compared to simpler RAG approaches.
*   **Potential Biases:** The new PubMed-Dialog dataset may have biases inherited from the source data and the GPT-4 generation process, as acknowledged by the authors.
*   **Lack of dynamic knowledge updates:** Future work should focus on the ability of the model to perform dynamic knowledge updates.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques: the information bottleneck for knowledge compression, the KA-Adapter for efficient integration, and the two-phase training approach. While retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) is not new, KEDiT provides a more efficient and adaptable fine-tuning method, particularly for incorporating domain-specific knowledge. The PubMed-Dialog dataset and the comprehensive evaluation also contribute to the field. The work addresses a critical need for LLMs to utilize current and specialized knowledge and offers a practical and scalable solution.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by presenting an effective and efficient approach for fine-tuning LLMs for knowledge-grounded dialogue generation. The combination of information bottleneck and lightweight adapter addresses key challenges in utilizing external knowledge. While the approach has some limitations (as noted above), the thorough evaluation, the new dataset, and the efficiency gains make it a valuable contribution to the field. The paper provides a solid foundation for future research on knowledge-grounded dialogue systems.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Exploring a Patch-Wise Approach for Privacy-Preserving Fake ID Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07761v1)
- **Authors**: Javier Muñoz-Haro, Ruben Tolosana, Ruben Vera-Rodriguez, Aythami Morales, Julian Fierrez
- **Abstract**: In an increasingly digitalized world, verifying the authenticity of ID documents has become a critical challenge for real-life applications such as digital banking, crypto-exchanges, renting, etc. This study focuses on the topic of fake ID detection, covering several limitations in the field. In particular, no publicly available data from real ID documents exists, and most studies rely on proprietary in-house databases that are not available due to privacy reasons. In order to shed some light on this critical challenge that makes difficult to advance in the field, we explore a trade-off between privacy (i.e., amount of sensitive data available) and performance, proposing a novel patch-wise approach for privacy-preserving fake ID detection. Our proposed approach explores how privacy can be enhanced through: i) two levels of anonymization for an ID document (i.e., fully- and pseudo-anonymized), and ii) different patch size configurations, varying the amount of sensitive data visible in the patch image. Also, state-of-the-art methods such as Vision Transformers and Foundation Models are considered in the analysis. The experimental framework shows that, on an unseen database (DLC-2021), our proposal achieves 13.91% and 0% EERs at patch and ID document level, showing a good generalization to other databases. In addition to this exploration, another key contribution of our study is the release of the first publicly available database that contains 48,400 patches from both real and fake ID documents, along with the experimental framework and models, which will be available in our GitHub.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores a patch-wise approach for privacy-preserving fake ID detection.  It addresses the lack of publicly available real ID datasets due to privacy concerns by proposing a method that operates on anonymized patches of ID documents.  The authors investigate two levels of anonymization (fully and pseudo), varying patch sizes, and leveraging state-of-the-art deep learning models like Vision Transformers (ViT) and Foundation Models (DINOv2) to classify patches as real or fake.  The paper contributes a publicly available database of 48,400 patches from real and fake ID documents and shows good generalization on the DLC-2021 dataset, achieving 13.91% EER at patch level and 0% EER at ID document level.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The patch-wise approach to fake ID detection, with a focus on privacy, is a worthwhile contribution.  While patch-based methods aren't entirely new in image analysis, their application to fake ID detection with explicit consideration for privacy through anonymization is a novel angle. The public dataset is a significant contribution, addressing a major barrier to research in this area. The exploration of different anonymization levels and patch sizes to strike a balance between privacy and performance is also valuable.

*   **Significance:** The research addresses a real-world problem with significant security implications (fraud, KYC verification). The lack of public datasets has severely hampered progress in this field, making the release of the authors' dataset a major step forward.  The good generalization results on the DLC-2021 database suggest the method has practical potential beyond the authors' curated dataset. The emphasis on privacy is especially important, as it allows for research and development in a sensitive domain while minimizing risks to individuals' personal information.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Public Dataset:** The primary strength is the release of a labeled dataset of ID document patches.  This is a critical resource for the community.
    *   **Privacy Focus:** Explicit consideration and evaluation of privacy trade-offs (anonymization levels, patch sizes) is excellent.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The paper explores a variety of configurations (patch sizes, anonymization, deep learning architectures) and provides a thorough performance analysis.
    *   **Good Generalization:** Achieving strong results on an unseen dataset (DLC-2021) demonstrates the potential applicability of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset Size:** While a valuable contribution, the dataset size (30 real IDs) is still relatively small. A larger, more diverse dataset would strengthen the impact and generalizability of the findings. The paper could benefit from a sensitivity analysis regarding the size of the real dataset.
    *   **Limited Types of Fakes:** The focus on print and screen attacks is good, but the rapid evolution of fake ID technology means that more sophisticated attacks (e.g., advanced fabrication techniques) need to be considered.
    *   **Potential for Reconstruction:** Although measures were taken to prevent ID reconstruction, the authors should acknowledge this concern remains and explore more advanced privacy-preserving techniques (e.g., differential privacy, federated learning) in future work.
    *   **Simple Fusion Technique:** A simple mean score aggregation is used for patch fusion to generate the ID score. The exploration of more advanced fusion methods should be a focus in the future.

*   **Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a tangible and valuable contribution by addressing a key bottleneck (lack of data) in an important security domain while also addressing privacy concerns. The experiments are comprehensive and demonstrate a solid technical approach. However, the relatively small dataset size and a limited set of PAI types does cap the score. While the work is impactful, further expansion of the dataset and exploration of advanced privacy techniques are necessary to realize its full potential. The selection of a simple fusion technique to generate the ID score can be improved with future research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fairness Mediator: Neutralize Stereotype Associations to Mitigate Bias in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07787v1)
- **Authors**: Yisong Xiao, Aishan Liu, Siyuan Liang, Xianglong Liu, Dacheng Tao
- **Abstract**: LLMs have demonstrated remarkable performance across diverse applications, yet they inadvertently absorb spurious correlations from training data, leading to stereotype associations between biased concepts and specific social groups. These associations perpetuate and even amplify harmful social biases, raising significant fairness concerns. To mitigate such biases, prior studies have attempted to project model embeddings into unbiased spaces during inference. However, these approaches have shown limited effectiveness due to their weak alignment with downstream social biases. Inspired by the observation that concept cognition in LLMs is primarily represented through a linear associative memory mechanism, where key-value mapping occurs in the MLP layers, we posited that biased concepts and social groups are similarly encoded as entity (key) and information (value) pairs, which can be manipulated to promote fairer associations. To this end, we propose Fairness Mediator (FairMed), a bias mitigation framework that neutralizes stereotype associations. Our framework comprises two main components: a stereotype association prober and an adversarial debiasing neutralizer. The prober captures stereotype associations encoded within MLP layer activations by employing prompts centered around biased concepts to detect the emission probabilities for social groups. Subsequently, the adversarial debiasing neutralizer intervenes in MLP activations during inference to equalize the association probabilities among different social groups. Extensive experiments across nine protected attributes show that FairMed significantly outperforms SOTA methods in effectiveness. Compared to the most effective baseline, FairMed presents competitive efficiency by cutting mitigation overhead by hundreds of minutes. FairMed also maintains the LLM's language understanding capabilities without compromising overall performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Fairness Mediator" (FairMed), a framework designed to mitigate stereotype associations in Large Language Models (LLMs) during inference. FairMed tackles the issue of LLMs perpetuating harmful biases by encoding stereotypes learned from training data. The framework comprises two components: a stereotype association prober, which identifies encoded biases within the activations of Multi-Layer Perceptron (MLP) layers, and an adversarial debiasing neutralizer, which manipulates these activations during inference to equalize association probabilities among different social groups. The authors hypothesize that biased concepts are encoded as key-value pairs within the MLP layers and can be adjusted to promote fairer associations.  The paper presents extensive experiments using LLaMA family models, demonstrating that FairMed outperforms several state-of-the-art methods in reducing bias, is computationally efficient, and maintains the LLM's language understanding capabilities. The authors also conduct ablation studies and generalization tests using other LLMs (BERT and BART) and datasets (BiasAsker and Adult).

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a somewhat novel approach by targeting the MLP activations directly, drawing an analogy between LLM concept encoding and key-value memory systems.  The use of adversarial debiasing at the activation level, guided by a prober trained to detect stereotype associations, distinguishes this method from prior techniques that often focus on projecting embeddings into unbiased spaces or modifying prompts. The observation that stereotypes are primarily encoded in middle to deeper layers of MLP networks is interesting and empirically validated.
* **Significance:** Addressing fairness in LLMs is a critical area of research, especially within software engineering contexts where these models are increasingly deployed. The paper’s claims of improved effectiveness and efficiency compared to existing methods are significant, suggesting a potential pathway to practical bias mitigation. The ability to maintain language understanding capabilities while reducing bias is also essential for real-world applications. The generalizability across multiple model families and datasets further strengthens the importance of the contribution.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides extensive experimental results on multiple datasets and LLM architectures. This demonstrates the effectiveness and generalizability of the proposed method.
    * **Efficiency:**  The paper emphasizes the efficiency of FairMed, showing that it significantly reduces training time compared to other approaches like CDA. This is an important factor for practical deployment.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The authors conduct thorough ablation studies to understand the impact of different hyperparameters and design choices. They also assess the impact of FairMed on language understanding capabilities.
    * **Clear Problem Definition and Methodology:** The paper clearly defines the problem of stereotype associations and presents a well-structured methodology for addressing it.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty in Techniques:** The techniques used, while combined in a novel manner, rely on established concepts: adversarial attacks, key-value stores, and MLP networks. The novelty lies more in the application of these ideas to the specific problem of bias in LLMs.
    * **Dependency on Model Accessibility:** FairMed requires access to internal model activations, which makes it a white-box method. This limits its applicability in scenarios where only API access to the LLM is available.
    * **Potential for Over-Correction:** The paper acknowledges that FairMed can lead to over-correction in some cases, reducing accuracy. While the accuracy reduction is smaller than some baselines, this remains a concern.
    * **Incremental Improvement:** While significant improvement over baselines, the improvements are relatively small, suggesting that the approach may not be effective for all biases. This can be further improved by exploring other areas in the model.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by offering a new perspective on bias mitigation in LLMs. It suggests that targeting MLP activations directly can be an effective strategy. The findings regarding stereotype encoding in MLP layers could inspire further research into the internal workings of LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of fairness in AI. It is well-researched, empirically validated, and clearly written. However, the core techniques are not radically new, and the white-box nature of the method restricts its scope. While the performance gains are notable, the limitations related to over-correction and the reliance on existing techniques prevent a higher score. There's room for further innovation in making the approach more generalizable (black-box) and further optimizing the debiasing process to minimize accuracy loss.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Breaking the Barriers: Video Vision Transformers for Word-Level Sign Language Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07792v1)
- **Authors**: Alexander Brettmann, Jakob Grävinghoff, Marlene Rüschoff, Marie Westhues
- **Abstract**: Sign language is a fundamental means of communication for the deaf and hard-of-hearing (DHH) community, enabling nuanced expression through gestures, facial expressions, and body movements. Despite its critical role in facilitating interaction within the DHH population, significant barriers persist due to the limited fluency in sign language among the hearing population. Overcoming this communication gap through automatic sign language recognition (SLR) remains a challenge, particularly at a dynamic word-level, where temporal and spatial dependencies must be effectively recognized. While Convolutional Neural Networks have shown potential in SLR, they are computationally intensive and have difficulties in capturing global temporal dependencies between video sequences. To address these limitations, we propose a Video Vision Transformer (ViViT) model for word-level American Sign Language (ASL) recognition. Transformer models make use of self-attention mechanisms to effectively capture global relationships across spatial and temporal dimensions, which makes them suitable for complex gesture recognition tasks. The VideoMAE model achieves a Top-1 accuracy of 75.58% on the WLASL100 dataset, highlighting its strong performance compared to traditional CNNs with 65.89%. Our study demonstrates that transformer-based architectures have great potential to advance SLR, overcome communication barriers and promote the inclusion of DHH individuals.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Breaking the Barriers: Video Vision Transformers for Word-Level Sign Language Recognition" explores the use of Video Vision Transformers (ViViTs) for word-level American Sign Language (ASL) recognition.  The authors address the communication gap between hearing and DHH individuals, highlighting the need for improved SLR systems. They propose using ViViT models, specifically TimeSformer and VideoMAE, leveraging their ability to capture global spatiotemporal relationships in video sequences. These models are fine-tuned on a subset (WLASL100) of the larger WLASL2000 dataset, and their performance is compared against a fine-tuned I3D model.  The results demonstrate that VideoMAE outperforms both TimeSformer and I3D on the WLASL100 dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the importance of SLR and the challenges faced by the DHH community.
    *   **Transformer-Based Approach:** It introduces a relevant and promising approach (ViViTs) to address limitations of CNN-based methods in capturing long-range dependencies for SLR.
    *   **Solid Experimental Results:** The paper presents experimental results showing VideoMAE's superiority over I3D on the WLASL100 dataset. An ablation study provides insights into hyperparameter influence.
    *   **Well-Structured and Readable:** The paper is well-organized, making it easy to follow the methodology and results.
    *   **Addresses a real-world and socially relevant problem** SLR enhances accessibility and inclusion.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty:** While applying ViViTs to SLR is a worthwhile exploration, the core architecture isn't fundamentally novel to the paper. The paper uses existing, pre-trained models and focuses primarily on fine-tuning. This diminishes the contribution from an algorithmic perspective. There is no introduction of a novel ViViT architecture or specific loss function/training regime tailored to SLR.
    *   **Dataset Size:** The use of a smaller subset (WLASL100) for computational feasibility is understandable, but it limits the scope of the study and generalizability. Applying and validating the models on larger datasets with more complex sign sequences and vocabulary sizes would significantly increase the impact.
    *   **Lack of Detailed Analysis:**  While the paper mentions attention maps, the qualitative analysis and interpretation of the learned features are limited. More detailed exploration of *why* VideoMAE performs better, by analyzing what features are being attended to, would be beneficial. A rigorous evaluation of failure cases could also provide insights to improve the model in future works.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:**  The paper claims 'superior accuracy' of VideoMAE over I3D, but the margin of improvement might not be drastically significant in the grand scheme of things.
    *   **Limited Comparisons:** The paper compares to a fine-tuned I3D model, but it lacks comparison to more recent state-of-the-art CNN or Hybrid models that might present stronger baseline performance on video action recognition or sign language recognition tasks.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper demonstrates the potential of transformers for SLR, motivating further research in this direction.
    *   It provides empirical evidence supporting the use of VideoMAE for capturing spatiotemporal dynamics in sign language videos.
    *   It contributes to the development of more effective SLR systems that can help bridge communication barriers.
    *   The results could provide an avenue to explore how transformer based models can be further refined to work with SLR

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper presents a useful application of ViViTs to a real-world problem (SLR) with promising results. However, the limited algorithmic novelty and the restricted dataset size constrain the overall impact. The paper serves as a good starting point but requires further development and validation on larger and more diverse datasets, as well as more detailed analyses, to truly advance the field.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

A score of 6 is appropriate because the paper demonstrates a practical application of ViViTs to SLR, showing that it's a viable direction. However, the limited novelty in the architecture and the scale of the experiments (WLASL100) mean the contribution is incremental rather than groundbreaking. The analysis and comparison to state-of-the-art is limited. This prevents a score of 7 or higher. Conversely, the clear methodology and reasonably good results (especially compared to I3D) justify a score higher than 5, indicating that it is a solid, if not transformative, contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Revisiting Likelihood-Based Out-of-Distribution Detection by Modeling Representations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07793v1)
- **Authors**: Yifan Ding, Arturas Aleksandrauskas, Amirhossein Ahmadian, Jonas Unger, Fredrik Lindsten, Gabriel Eilertsen
- **Abstract**: Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection is critical for ensuring the reliability of deep learning systems, particularly in safety-critical applications. Likelihood-based deep generative models have historically faced criticism for their unsatisfactory performance in OOD detection, often assigning higher likelihood to OOD data than in-distribution samples when applied to image data. In this work, we demonstrate that likelihood is not inherently flawed. Rather, several properties in the images space prohibit likelihood as a valid detection score. Given a sufficiently good likelihood estimator, specifically using the probability flow formulation of a diffusion model, we show that likelihood-based methods can still perform on par with state-of-the-art methods when applied in the representation space of pre-trained encoders. The code of our work can be found at $\href{https://github.com/limchaos/Likelihood-OOD.git}{\texttt{https://github.com/limchaos/Likelihood-OOD.git}}$.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Revisiting Likelihood-Based Out-of-Distribution Detection by Modeling Representations":

**Summary:**

This paper challenges the long-held belief that likelihood-based methods are fundamentally flawed for out-of-distribution (OOD) detection, particularly in the image domain. It argues that the unsatisfactory performance previously observed stems from applying likelihood estimation directly to image pixels, which is heavily influenced by low-level features and background statistics. The authors propose estimating likelihood in the representation space of pre-trained image encoders using a score-based diffusion model. They demonstrate that this approach achieves results comparable to or better than state-of-the-art OOD detection methods, especially when combined with supervised pre-training and class-conditional guidance for the diffusion model. The study conducts extensive experiments on ImageNet and histopathology datasets, evaluating supervised and self-supervised encoders.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in its *revisitation* of likelihood-based OOD detection. While diffusion models and representation learning are established techniques, the specific combination and the insightful diagnosis of why likelihood estimation fails in pixel space represent a non-trivial contribution. By shifting the focus to the representation space, the authors overcome limitations faced by earlier pixel-based likelihood methods. The use of class-conditional diffusion models to further improve OOD detection performance also adds to the novelty. However, the individual components are well-established, which limits the overall novelty somewhat.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a significant problem in machine learning: reliable OOD detection, which is crucial for safe and robust AI systems. The finding that likelihood-based methods can be highly effective in the representation space has potentially far-reaching implications. It provides a practical and potentially scalable alternative to existing OOD detection techniques, particularly in settings where labeled data is limited (due to the applicability of self-supervised encoders). This could be particularly valuable for specialized domains like medical imaging, as highlighted in the histopathology experiments. The competitive performance achieved, coupled with the inherent interpretability of likelihood, makes this work impactful.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition and Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of OOD detection and the historical shortcomings of likelihood-based methods. The motivation for moving to representation space is well-explained.
    * **Thorough Experiments:** The paper presents extensive experimental results on large-scale benchmarks (ImageNet) and a clinically relevant dataset (histopathology). A wide range of encoders, both supervised and self-supervised, are evaluated, demonstrating the generalizability of the approach.
    * **Detailed Ablation Studies:** Although not explicitly stated, comparing the performances of models with/without label information allows ablation of effects.
    * **Reproducibility:** The availability of code is excellent.
    * **Well-Written and Structured:** The paper is well-organized, easy to follow, and presents the arguments in a logical manner.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the paper provides a convincing empirical demonstration, there's a lack of deep theoretical analysis regarding the properties of representation spaces that enable effective likelihood estimation.  A more rigorous discussion of why certain representation spaces are better suited for this approach would strengthen the work. Why, specifically, CNNs produce inferior representations?
    * **Reliance on Pre-trained Encoders:** The approach relies on the quality of pre-trained encoders. While this is often practical, it adds a dependency. The results are intrinsically linked to the quality of encoders.
    * **Limited Comparison for Self-Supervised Methods:** In the self-supervised encoder experiments, the comparison is limited. Are there more advanced OOD detection specifically designed for self-supervised representations?
    * **Limited Discussion of Computational Cost:** While the paper mentions computational efficiency, a more detailed comparison of the computational cost relative to other state-of-the-art OOD detection methods would be beneficial.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to shift research focus back to likelihood-based methods for OOD detection by demonstrating their effectiveness in representation space. It could inspire further research into designing or fine-tuning encoders to create representation spaces optimized for OOD detection. The approach could be readily adopted in practical applications, especially in safety-critical domains.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution to the field. The revisiting of a previously dismissed approach, the insightful analysis of the challenges in pixel space, and the strong experimental results justify a good score. The potential for practical impact and influence on future research is significant. However, the incremental nature of the novelty (relying on established techniques), the lack of deep theoretical analysis, and some limitations in the experimental comparison prevent it from achieving a higher score. Nevertheless, the work is well-executed, thoroughly validated, and clearly presented.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Plan-and-Refine: Diverse and Comprehensive Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07794v1)
- **Authors**: Alireza Salemi, Chris Samarinas, Hamed Zamani
- **Abstract**: This paper studies the limitations of (retrieval-augmented) large language models (LLMs) in generating diverse and comprehensive responses, and introduces the Plan-and-Refine (P&R) framework based on a two phase system design. In the global exploration phase, P&R generates a diverse set of plans for the given input, where each plan consists of a list of diverse query aspects with corresponding additional descriptions. This phase is followed by a local exploitation phase that generates a response proposal for the input query conditioned on each plan and iteratively refines the proposal for improving the proposal quality. Finally, a reward model is employed to select the proposal with the highest factuality and coverage. We conduct our experiments based on the ICAT evaluation methodology--a recent approach for answer factuality and comprehensiveness evaluation. Experiments on the two diverse information seeking benchmarks adopted from non-factoid question answering and TREC search result diversification tasks demonstrate that P&R significantly outperforms baselines, achieving up to a 13.1% improvement on the ANTIQUE dataset and a 15.41% improvement on the TREC dataset. Furthermore, a smaller scale user study confirms the substantial efficacy of the P&R framework.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Plan-and-Refine (P&R) framework to address the limitations of standard retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) models in producing diverse and comprehensive responses. P&R is a two-phase system. The global exploration phase generates a diverse set of plans, each outlining key query aspects and their relevance. The local exploitation phase then uses each plan to generate an initial response and iteratively refine it for improved quality and factuality. Finally, a reward model selects the best response based on coverage and factuality. The approach is evaluated on non-factoid question answering (ANTIQUE) and TREC search result diversification tasks, demonstrating improvements over baseline RAG models.  A user study also confirms user preference for P&R-generated responses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The P&R framework possesses a decent level of novelty. While retrieval augmentation is a well-established technique, the explicit separation into a planning and refinement stage, along with the integration of techniques to improve both response diversity and individual response quality, constitutes a non-trivial contribution. The combination of self-training for plan generation and iterative refinement using an LLM is a reasonably novel combination. However, the individual components, such as iterative refinement or self-training, are not groundbreaking in isolation.

*   **Significance and Impact:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in the field of text generation: ensuring diversity and comprehensiveness in RAG-based systems. The work's potential impact stems from the fact that many real-world applications (e.g., information seeking, customer service) demand not just accurate responses but also responses that address all relevant aspects of a query. The demonstrated improvements on established benchmarks are significant and should spark further research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The P&R framework is well-motivated, clearly explained, and easy to understand.
    *   The experimental evaluation is rigorous, including comparisons against strong baselines, ablation studies to assess the contribution of individual components, and a user study.
    *   The results demonstrate statistically significant improvements on two relevant benchmark datasets.
    *   The public release of the code promotes reproducibility and facilitates further research.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The use of a reward model adds complexity and requires additional training. The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the reward model's impact and potential biases.
    *   The results are somewhat specific to the chosen datasets and tasks. Further evaluation on a broader range of text generation problems would strengthen the generalizability of the P&R framework.
    *   While the user study is useful, it's limited in scale. A larger study with more diverse participants would increase confidence in the findings.
    *   The implementation depends on a number of hyperparameters (number of plans, refining steps, temperature settings for sampling etc.) A sensitivity analysis on the impact of these parameters would be useful.
    *   The efficiency of the P&R is not addressed adequately. The paper is not clear about the computational demands of planning.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper provides a solid, incremental improvement over existing RAG models. It's technically sound, well-evaluated, and addresses an important problem in the field. The novelty is adequate, and the potential impact is good, but the dependence on a reward model and lack of strong evaluation of that component limit the score.
