- **Score**: 7/10

### PixelFlow: Pixel-Space Generative Models with Flow
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07963v1)
- **Authors**: Shoufa Chen, Chongjian Ge, Shilong Zhang, Peize Sun, Ping Luo
- **Abstract**: We present PixelFlow, a family of image generation models that operate directly in the raw pixel space, in contrast to the predominant latent-space models. This approach simplifies the image generation process by eliminating the need for a pre-trained Variational Autoencoder (VAE) and enabling the whole model end-to-end trainable. Through efficient cascade flow modeling, PixelFlow achieves affordable computation cost in pixel space. It achieves an FID of 1.98 on 256$\times$256 ImageNet class-conditional image generation benchmark. The qualitative text-to-image results demonstrate that PixelFlow excels in image quality, artistry, and semantic control. We hope this new paradigm will inspire and open up new opportunities for next-generation visual generation models. Code and models are available at https://github.com/ShoufaChen/PixelFlow.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the PixelFlow paper:

**Summary:**

The PixelFlow paper introduces a novel image generation framework that operates directly in the pixel space, contrasting with the prevalent latent diffusion models (LDMs) that require a pre-trained Variational Autoencoder (VAE). PixelFlow simplifies the generation process by enabling end-to-end training and utilizes a cascaded flow modeling technique to achieve affordable computational costs in the high-dimensional pixel space. The approach involves progressively increasing resolution during denoising, starting from a low-resolution image and scaling it up as noise is reduced. The paper demonstrates PixelFlow's capabilities on class-conditional ImageNet generation and text-to-image generation tasks, achieving competitive performance and showing strong visual fidelity and semantic control.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The most significant novelty of this paper lies in its departure from the standard latent space diffusion paradigm.  While pixel-space diffusion models are not entirely new, the cascaded flow modeling approach within pixel space, enabling end-to-end training *and* achieving competitive results, is a substantial contribution. The use of Flow Matching for image generation, while not completely novel in itself, is applied in a unique way within the cascaded framework. Combining this with a carefully designed multi-scale architecture and a specific training scheme to stabilize end-to-end training adds to the novelty.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work is multifaceted:

*   **Simplification:** Eliminating the VAE simplifies the generative model pipeline, reducing complexity and enabling joint optimization. This is beneficial for training and diagnosis.
*   **Performance:** Achieving competitive FID scores on ImageNet and promising text-to-image generation results validates the viability of direct pixel-space generation, challenging the established LDM dominance. The strong qualitative results and visual fidelity suggest an advantage in capturing fine-grained details, which is often a weakness of VAE-based latent models.
*   **Potential for Future Research:** The paper opens up new research directions by demonstrating a viable alternative to LDMs. The end-to-end trainability and focus on pixel space could lead to improved control over generation and the development of models that better preserve image fidelity. The multi-scale generation process could also be improved by utilizing different architectural designs and optimization methods.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** While the cascaded flow modeling makes pixel-space generation *affordable*, it likely still carries a higher computational burden than LDMs, especially during inference where the final stage demands a lot of computational power. The paper should have included a detailed study on the computational trade-off.
*   **Longer Training:** The paper indicates that training convergence slows as sequence length increases, which means that the training process requires specific training techniques and parameter tuning.
*   **Limited Scope:**  While the results on ImageNet and text-to-image are promising, further evaluation on more diverse datasets and tasks is required to fully assess the generalizability and robustness of PixelFlow. It would also be useful to assess the model's ability to perform complex edits or manipulations compared to latent space methods.
*   **Ablation Studies:** Although the paper includes ablation studies (kickoff sequence length, patch size, etc.), a broader investigation into the architectural choices or hyperparameter settings may improve the model's efficiency.

**Overall Assessment:**

PixelFlow presents a valuable contribution to the field of generative modeling. The shift away from VAE-based latent spaces and towards direct pixel-space generation is a compelling direction, and the paper demonstrates a concrete and effective approach. The competitive results achieved by PixelFlow validate its potential and open up avenues for future research to further improve its efficiency and capabilities. However, the computational cost and other weaknesses need to be addressed in future studies. The paper demonstrates an impressive effort, offering a pathway to enhance image generation through novel architectural techniques and training optimizations in raw pixel space, paving the way for improvements in visual generative modeling.

**Score: 7.5**

The score is justified because while the approach has novelty and significance, there are aspects that could use improvements, such as computational cost, training convergence, and more extensive comparisons. These limitations keep the score from going into a higher range.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### C3PO: Critical-Layer, Core-Expert, Collaborative Pathway Optimization for Test-Time Expert Re-Mixing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07964v1)
- **Authors**: Zhongyang Li, Ziyue Li, Tianyi Zhou
- **Abstract**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs) suffer from severely sub-optimal expert pathways-our study reveals that naive expert selection learned from pretraining leaves a surprising 10-20% accuracy gap for improvement. Motivated by this observation, we develop a novel class of test-time optimization methods to re-weight or "re-mixing" the experts in different layers jointly for each test sample. Since the test sample's ground truth is unknown, we propose to optimize a surrogate objective defined by the sample's "successful neighbors" from a reference set of samples. We introduce three surrogates and algorithms based on mode-finding, kernel regression, and the average loss of similar reference samples/tasks. To reduce the cost of optimizing whole pathways, we apply our algorithms merely to the core experts' mixing weights in critical layers, which enjoy similar performance but save significant computation. This leads to "Critical-Layer, Core-Expert, Collaborative Pathway Optimization (C3PO)". We apply C3PO to two recent MoE LLMs and examine it on six widely-used benchmarks. It consistently improves the base model by 7-15% in accuracy and outperforms widely used test-time learning baselines, e.g., in-context learning and prompt/prefix tuning, by a large margin. Moreover, C3PO enables MoE LLMs with 1-3B active parameters to outperform LLMs of 7-9B parameters, hence improving MoE's advantages on efficiency. Our thorough ablation study further sheds novel insights on achieving test-time improvement on MoE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the research paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces C3PO (Critical-Layer, Core-Expert, Collaborative Pathway Optimization), a novel test-time optimization method designed to improve the performance of Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs).  The key idea is to re-weight or "re-mix" the experts in different layers of the MoE model for each specific test sample.  Since ground truth labels are not available at test time, C3PO optimizes a surrogate objective by leveraging "successful neighbors" from a reference set of samples. The paper proposes three different surrogate objectives and algorithms (mode finding, kernel regression, and weighted loss sum) to achieve this. To reduce computational cost, the optimization is limited to critical layers and core experts within those layers. The authors demonstrate C3PO's effectiveness on several MoE models and benchmark datasets, showing significant accuracy improvements over baseline models and other test-time adaptation methods like in-context learning and prompt/prefix tuning.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:*

The core idea of test-time expert re-mixing in MoE models is interesting and represents a relatively unexplored area.  The identification of suboptimal pathways in existing MoE LLMs as a key problem is a valuable observation. The introduction of surrogate objectives based on neighboring successful samples is clever and addresses the issue of lacking ground truth at test time. Focusing optimization on critical layers and core experts to reduce computational cost is a practical and well-justified contribution.

*Significance:*

The demonstrated performance gains are significant, achieving 7-15% accuracy improvements on various benchmarks.  The ability for smaller MoE models (with fewer active parameters) to outperform larger, dense models showcases the potential of C3PO to improve the efficiency and effectiveness of MoE architectures.  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the factors influencing test-time improvements in MoEs.  The method effectively bridges the gap in test-time adaptation for MoE models, an area where current techniques are limited.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the sub-optimality of expert pathways in MoE models as a critical limitation.
*   **Novel Approach:**  C3PO offers a novel test-time optimization method specific to MoE architectures, addressing a gap in existing techniques.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental validation on multiple datasets and models, demonstrating the effectiveness of C3PO.
*   **Efficient Optimization:** The critical layer and core expert strategies significantly reduce the computational cost of pathway optimization.
*   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the design choices and parameters influencing the performance of C3PO.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Reference Set Dependency:** Performance is highly dependent on the quality and representativeness of the reference set. The selection process and impact of reference set size could be explored more thoroughly. The sensitivity of the method to distribution shift between the reference set and the test data is a potential concern that should be discussed.
*   **Computational Overhead:** Although the authors mention reduction of computational costs, the actual overhead introduced by C3PO is not clearly quantified. A detailed comparison of inference time with and without C3PO would be beneficial.
*   **Limited Model Variety:**  Experiments are primarily focused on two MoE models (OLMoE and DeepSeekMoE). Applying C3PO to a wider range of MoE architectures would further strengthen the results.
*   **Black-Box Nature:**  While the paper demonstrates C3PO's effectiveness, it provides limited insights into *why* specific expert re-mixings are beneficial for particular samples or tasks.  A deeper analysis of the learned pathways could provide a more intuitive understanding of the method.

*Potential Influence:*

C3PO has the potential to significantly impact the research and development of MoE models.  It opens up new avenues for test-time adaptation and optimization, potentially leading to more efficient and effective deployment of MoE architectures in real-world applications. The insights gained from the ablation studies could also inform the design of future MoE models and routing mechanisms.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a strong and novel approach with significant empirical results, the limitations related to reference set dependency, computational overhead, and limited model variety prevent it from achieving a higher score. The lack of deeper interpretability regarding the benefits of specific pathway adjustments also holds it back. However, the practical value and potential impact on MoE model research warrant a score that acknowledges the importance of its contributions.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10
